- 2026-10-02 10:01 · 018 review changes · by rev-runes-018 (fresh) · Important: part 2 moves type-parameter uses down a line, against the bar 'every declaration and use keeps its true line'. constructorJoins moves the newline into the blank after 'class', so the name AND its type parameters drop to the constructor's row. Repro: class Outer { class Box<T : com.acme.Bound> / private constructor(val t: T) } gives the use com.acme.Bound at line 4, but the true line is 3. class Box<@com.acme.Ann T> does the same. The doc comment on constructorJoins and the commit body both claim 'every declaration and use keeps its line', which is false for these. Cheap fix: skip the join when the type-parameter list holds anything beyond identifiers, commas and in/out (a ':' bound, an '@' annotation, a dotted name), so that form fails closed as before. Add a test that checks the line of a bound's use.

- 2026-10-02 10:05 · 016 review changes · by rev-runes-016 (fresh) · Important: the new declaration_expression branch opens a false-edge path on valid C#. Per C# spec 6.2.5 an argument pair 'F(i < Order, j > Max)' is two relational expressions (an identifier after '>' does not disambiguate outside is/case/out/tuple), but tree-sitter-c_sharp reads it as one declaration_expression 'i<Order, j> Max', and the walk now emits i, Order and j as type references. Repro <the session scratchpad, since lost>/patch-611/review-016-csharp-refs/after/probe/fp.mjs: class with 'int Order => 1' calling 'Within(i < Order, j > Max)' under 'using Shop.Core;' with Shop.Core.Order in another node: base [] , branch edge app -> core at line 7. Same for constructor and indexer argument lists (new P(i < Count, j > Max), this[a < B, c > D]). Fix suggestion: read a declaration_expression's type only when its argument carries 'out' or it sits in a tuple (deconstruction); a bare argument of an argument_list/bracketed_argument_list is the misreading. Add a silence catalogue case for it. (The qualified form 'Check(x.i < Count, j > Max)' already emitted before 016 through the generic qualified_name pass; that pre-existing half can be its own issue.)
- 2026-10-02 10:05 · 075 review changes · by rev-runes-016 (fresh) · Important: part a regresses an edge 1.0.0 reports. When a web node declares the global Program explicitly ('public partial class Program { }', the documented WebApplicationFactory pattern for .NET 6-9) and ANY other node has top-level statements (Aspire AppHost, a worker, a migrator), the implicit key from that other file makes 'Program' two-owner ambiguous and the test -> web edge goes silent. Repro <the session scratchpad, since lost>/patch-611/review-016-csharp-refs/after/probe/partial.mjs (web top-level + partial, apphost top-level, tests WebApplicationFactory<Program>): base edge tests -> web at ApiTests.cs:4, branch []. Real repo: bug-hunt verify-44 ca-partial-web (CleanArchitecture with the partial in Web) under yg 6.1.0: base reports Application-FunctionalTests -> Web at WebApiFactory.cs:10, the branch's csharp.mjs drops it. The same holds for a classic global 'class Program { static Main }' next to any top-level file elsewhere. This is a behaviour trade-off patch-scope sends to the owner, and the changelog fragment hides it ('stays silent' reads as no change). Either avoid it (let the implicit Program yield to an explicit global Program, e.g. publish it under an internal fallback key that the bare 'Program' reference tries only after the verbatim key is absent; no new export needed), or ask the owner and state the lost edge in the fragment.

- 2026-10-02 10:17 · 011 review changes · by rev-runes-010 (fresh) · Important: holds() passes the default include (no files, no include) through asDirectory(configDir), and asDirectory treats a last segment with a dot as a file pattern, so a config in a directory like apps/web.v2 holds nothing by default; tsc's default is **/* under the config and ignores the name. Effects, each checked against tsc 6.0.3 --listFilesOnly: (a) regression on a non-solution layout, a false edge: apps/web.v2/tsconfig.json with its own paths @/* -> ./src/*, no include, references ./tsconfig.node.json (include **/*.ts, paths -> ./build/*): 1.0.0 and tsc give apps/web.v2/src/m.ts, the branch gives apps/web.v2/build/m.ts; (b) a solution root in a dotted dir whose default-include config and a second config both hold the file with different paths: tsc has two disagreeing holders (ruling: silence), the branch binds the second; (c) a lone default-include referenced config in a dotted dir is never a holder (silence). Fix, verified to cure all three: const include = config.include ?? (config.files === undefined ? [configDir === '' ? '**/*' : `${configDir}/**/*`] : []); plus a unit test. Repro: scratchpad patch-611/review-010-ts-resolve/probe011/probe2.mjs (S14) and probe.mjs (S1, S5).

- 2026-10-02 10:48 · 149 review changes · by rev-runes-135 (fresh) · Important: CRLF regression in tomlLogicalLines + KEY_VALUE. A joined logical line keeps each line's \r (only the \n becomes a space), and KEY_VALUE's (.*)$ cannot cross a \r, so any CRLF entry whose inline table holds a multi-line array no longer matches at all. Before 5be4794 the first physical line was read; now the whole entry is dropped: kit = { workspace = true, features = [\r\n "x",\r\n] } (zed's crates/zed gpui_platform shape) or a path written before the array loses the crate, and the 149 shape itself (path after the array) is still silent on CRLF. Evidence: disk-backed CRLF workspace (<the session scratchpad, since lost>/patch-611/review-135-cargo-manifest/crlf-ws, crlf-edge.mjs): kit::Engine from crates/app/src/main.rs binds crates/kit/src/lib.rs on fbc0539 and is undefined on 462c70c. The worker's 344-file corpus converted to CRLF (diff-cargo-eol.mjs, corpus-crlf.log): base 2 disagreements with smol-toml, head 31 new (17 workspace deps in zed/Cargo.toml incl. reqwest, gpui_platform workspace=true in crates/zed, xim and 6 more in gpui_linux, ...); LF stays new=0 fixed=2. A Windows checkout with autocrlf gives exactly this. Fix: treat \r\n as the newline in tomlLogicalLines (or let the value capture [\s\S]*), plus a CRLF unit case (path before and after a multi-line array).

Reproduced at 220af73: origin https://user:SEKRET12/x@host.example/acme/app.git (raw / in the password, e.g. an unencoded base64 token) - new URL throws and the fallback regex stops at the first /; https:user:SEKRET30@host.example/acme/app.git and https:/user:SEKRET31@host.example/... (scheme without //) fall into the scp branch and are returned whole. Fix: fail closed - when the URL does not parse, or the scp form still has an @ before the host colon, store null (or host+path only); add these shapes to oracle-record-privacy.test.mjs.

- 2026-10-02 11:52 · 162 review changes · by rev-runes-165 (fresh) · Important: a [lib] path outside src/ at or below a [[bin]] directory hands the library's files to the binary (rustTargetFor's outside-src branch ignores libPath). Probes, base -> head: [lib] path=lib.rs + [[bin]] path=main.rs, crate::Config from library module foo.rs: lib.rs -> main.rs (decoy, main.rs shadows Config); crate::LibOnly: lib.rs -> undefined. [lib] path=lib/lib.rs + [[bin]] path=main.rs: crate::Item from lib/util.rs: lib/lib.rs -> main.rs (decoy); crate::util from lib/other.rs: lib/lib.rs -> util.rs (the binary's module, decoy). [lib] and [[bin]] both in crates/core: crate::Item from crates/core/util.rs: crates/core/lib.rs -> undefined. Fix: let the library root's directory compete in the deepest-directory choice (a file under the lib's directory keeps the library tree; same directory as a bin -> both roots, like src/'s [lib, main], or fail closed).

- 2026-10-02 12:11 · 048 review changes · by rev-grain-048 (fresh) · Important: cb3e416 moves file co-change for completeness, check, review, the hooks and where from the whole history to the newest 20 000 non-bulk commits. Before, model.cochange/nonMegaCommits/fileTouches came from the walk's running counts (history.mjs replay: state.pairSup/fileCommits/fileOthers, never truncated); now applyFileCochange counts H.fps, which history.mjs truncates to CFG.fpsCap = 20000. This is a new limit for these surfaces (grain cochange always had it). On any repository with more than 20 000 non-bulk commits, partners whose co-change is older than the window vanish and every count and base rate shifts. Shown on scratch copies of both trees with fpsCap lowered to 30 (x.js+y.js together 10 times, then 40 other commits): release/6.1.1 names y.js 11/11, the branch names nothing (scratch: <the session scratchpad, since lost>/patch-611/review-048-cochange-one-population, make-old.sh, cap30-*). Not asked by the acceptance, not raised to the owner (patch-scope: a behaviour change with a real trade-off goes to him as a question), absent from the changelog entry, and docs/mathematics.md:292 still says fpsCap is a compute guard on which no claim rests. An alternative exists: footprints list only the new path of a rename (fps {files:[mid/a.js], renames:[[src/a.js, mid/a.js]]}), so the walk's un-floored full-history counts could be carried through currentPathOf before the floor, with its own edge cases (a commit naming two historical paths that resolve to one). Either ask the owner and disclose the window in the changelog and mathematics.md, or keep the full-history population.

- 2026-10-02 12:47 · 104 review changes · by rev-grain-104 (fresh) · Important: the stack is read only from the edited file, and its head stops at the topmost decorator still there. So when an edit turns a declaration's only (or topmost) decorator into a comment or a blank line, or deletes it while a comment sits between it and the declaration, the declaration is no longer in the change and the deviation the edit made is filed as pre-existing. Fixture cart.handler.ts line 6 '@Handler()' -> '// @Handler()': before '1 deviation(s) in your change', and the check-hook says 'Your type CreateCartHandler (line 7) is not annotated with @Handler'; after '0 deviation(s) in your change, 1 pre-existing' and the hook is silent. The same happens when line 6 is blanked, and when '@Handler()' is deleted with '// eslint-disable-next-line ...' committed between it and the class. Real repo, flask d73fa1c tests/conftest.py:37 '@pytest.fixture' -> '# @pytest.fixture': before '1 deviation(s) in your change ... Your method app_ctx (line 38) is not annotated with @pytest.fixture' and the hook speaks; after '0 deviation(s) in your change, 2 pre-existing' and the hook gives only the co-change line. Acceptance 3 ('a change to or a deletion of the @Handler() itself is still attributed to the class') fails for the commonest way to switch a decorator off. By construction the same holds for Java/C#/Kotlin/PHP annotations: once commented out, the comment sits outside the declaration's node. The tests cover only a decorator swapped for another (D) and a deletion directly above the class (D2). Possible fix: also count a hunk whose removed lines lay on the scope's stack at HEAD (the pre-image), or let the head extend over the comment lines directly attached to it.

- 2026-10-02 20:48 · 003 evidence · grain-200: not fixed, per grain-scope (003 minor). Probe on HEAD: Grain's method population counts Java constructor_declaration (PascalCase, a camelCase deviant) and TS/JS arrow_function bindings; the rendered name-shape check reads only NT.funcDecl nodes with a name field, so it skips both. Aligning means choosing a direction: (a) Grain leaves language-fixed names (constructors) out of the method name-shape count, or (b) the check reads the node types Grain counted (export applicableNodeTypes, as returns does) — (b) alone would make yg refuse every Java constructor under a camelCase rule.

- 2026-10-02 20:55 · 202 review changes · by rev-grain-200 (fresh) · Important: ESM detection is 'top-level import/export or .mjs/.mts'. A TypeScript declaration file without import/export (global.d.ts, env.d.ts: 'declare global {...}', 'interface Foo {}') gets g=typescript, esm=false, directive='none'. It is therefore counted as a deviant of 'use strict' and is a carrier the adopted scope reaches. The result is a false-red after adoption on a file where the directive means nothing, and the changelog line 'no longer counts TypeScript ... files' is false for it. Fix: treat .d.ts (and arguably every TypeScript-grammar file, which tsc emits strict) as module-like, and add a test case.

- 2026-10-03 10:19 · 193 evidence · Ruling 193-tracked-cache-untrusted: 193: local cache/state under .yggdrasil/ tracked by git (index or HEAD) is never trusted — .yg-lock.deterministic.json included. Test 6A in cli-log-remaining, which claims that a committed verdict cache survives a revert, is rewritten (option a): revert of committed files only, no log-integrity, a clean free fill, check --approve exit 0. This closes a false green, so in line with ask-001 (owner: blocking right away with 'Before you upgrade') it goes in with a CHANGELOG note: a repository that commits .yg-lock.deterministic.json or another local cache will get, after upgrading, a warning and unverified script pairs — fix: add the file to .gitignore, remove it from the index (git rm --cached) and run the free yg check --approve --only-deterministic.

This narrows the literal wording of the acceptance for UTF-16 without a BOM (now only a warning, not relation-parse-failed): without a BOM, UTF-16 cannot be told apart from a binary file (e.g. a .ts video segment), and a patch must not turn a repository red for something that is not a defect. relation-parse-failed stays blocking for UTF-16 text with a BOM that still has NUL, and for a file whose tree is nothing but ERROR.

- 2026-10-03 11:21 · 193 review changes · by rev-ygg-063 (fresh) · Important (42753644c): failing closed turns a normal CI red. Reproduced in a scratch repo with a passing script rule and the yg init ignore lines, run by a different user so git reports dubious ownership (no chmod/chown used). On 0a011fd0b: yg check --approve --only-deterministic PASS, then yg check PASS. On 42753644c: the free fill itself exits 1 (error[unverified] 1 pair whose script check has not run), and yg check exits 1 too. The fill runs the script but writeOrRemoveSplitFile refuses to write .yg-lock.deterministic.json, so even this run's own verdicts are thrown away. A fresh container checkout with dubious ownership plus script rules is therefore always red. Suggest: when git can't be asked, distrust only what was on disk before the run (or skip just the read) while still writing and using this run's own verdicts. Or decide to accept the red and put it in the Before-you-upgrade note.

- 2026-10-03 12:41 · 008 evidence · Ruling 008-skip-parent-without-graph: 008: when comparing the progressive block with HEAD's parents, we skip a parent that has no yg-config.yaml (the commit/PR that adds the first graph). This goes slightly beyond the scope of yggdrasil-scope, but it keeps the promise of progressive mode (adoption does not turn all existing code red) and agrees with Grain 196's sentence: 'progressive mode starts once the adoption is committed on the local reference branch, or merged into origin/<branch>'. A PR adding the first graph with a reference pointing at itself passes the measured step; --full still catches it.

- 2026-10-03 12:47 · 069 review changes · by rev-ygg-069 (fresh) · Important: the content probe drops every UTF-16 text file with a byte-order mark from reviewer subjects (a NUL in the first 8 KiB). Reproduced on 1bac2e084: a node mapping q.sql (FF FE, UTF-16LE), be.cs (FE FF, UTF-16BE) and u8bom.cs gives the LLM pair subjects [u8bom.cs] only. In 6.1.0 those files reached the reviewer; now they leave silently, and a node holding only such files gets no pair (a vacuous pass, the class of fault Step 2.5 exists to prevent). This disagrees with 098, which reads FF FE/FE FF files as UTF-16 text and treats only NUL-without-BOM as binary. Fix: exempt a file starting with FF FE or FE FF from isBinarySubject (decoding it for the prompt is optional), and add a unit test for a UTF-16LE and a UTF-16BE subject that stays in. Ruling (a), dropping the second acceptance, is accepted.

- 2026-10-04 20:55 · 219 review changes · by rev-ygg-206 (fresh) · Important: adopt cannot tell its own .gitattributes from the owner's. Probe: the owner committed a .gitattributes with LFS and eol rules before adoption, under coverage.required [/] with no component mapping it. Adopt added it to coverage.excluded anyway, and the summary claims 'it holds the git settings this acceptance writes'. The owner's file then drops out of coverage permanently and without a real reason. Fix: exclude only when ensureGitattributes created the file (it did not exist) or when the file holds nothing but Yggdrasil's lines; otherwise leave it, so the unmapped report stays, or say plainly in the summary that the file is the owner's. Add a test for that case.
- 2026-10-04 20:55 · 189 review changes · by rev-ygg-206 (fresh) · Important: the new log-entry-missing is reported only by a plain check. Probe on the tip: a conflicted merge finished with git commit, then 'yg check --approve' run straight away exits 0, PASS, and closes the cycle. The following 'yg check' is PASS, the merged log keeps a branch's entry as its newest, and the owed entry was never asked for. That is the very 'easy to skip' of the Why. --approve has to refuse (or stop at) the same unreconciled merge the plain check flags.

- 2026-10-04 22:55 · 166 review changes · by rev-ygg-166 (fresh) · Important: nested re-include of a DIRECTORY is not honoured for files below it. Repro (from fuzz vs git check-ignore --no-index, git 2.55, tracked and untracked, ignorecase true and false): root .gitignore 'a/', Sub/.gitignore '!a/', file Sub/a/n.txt -> git keeps it, isFileGitignored=true and walkRepoFiles drops it. Same with root 'build/' + b/gen/.gitignore '!*/'. Cause: stackIgnores asks each layer about the leaf, and node-ignore's test() checks the leaf's parent dirs inside that shallow layer ('a/' matches), so the shallow layer's inherited verdict wins after the deeper layer already re-included the directory; the ancestor loop got the directory right. Fix direction: once the ancestor loop clears every ancestor, a layer's verdict that comes only from a parent directory must not count (e.g. skip a layer whose test(parentDir/) is ignored). Add that case to the parity test.

- 2026-10-05 14:58 · 221 review changes · by rev-ygg-221 (fresh) · Important — (b) is a false red: after a rebase, the entry the message asks for, committed without a fill (the documented log-cycle-open state), is still log-entry-missing in every clone, and yg check --approve refuses over it (reproduced: log add, commit, check FAIL, approve exit 1). To clear it the user has to write a second entry and fill before committing. The message says the newest entry 'was committed with that history rather than written for it', which is false here, and the CHANGELOG line 'an entry of its own settles it' is false once committed. The node log says a replayed entry and a later one cannot be told apart, but they can: a replayed entry predates the combining commit's committer date, and a later entry postdates it.
- 2026-10-05 14:58 · 222 review approve · by rev-ygg-221 (fresh) · No Critical or Important findings. The portal example now runs with --port 0, and the server prints the port it actually bound (server.address()). The test asserts that no example served on 4317, and the tolerance that accepted 'in use' is gone (the old regex never matched the CLI's 'is in use' anyway). No other test binds a fixed portal port. The cli-lock-scope budget of 180 s only stops a hung case: every assertion is unchanged, and the global testTimeout of 30000 still covers the rest; the other heavy e2e suites use 240 s. reference-truth and lock-scope pass 9/9 on the tip.

- 2026-10-08 11:45 · asked a-018 (charter) · 6.2.0 growth, all of it in one question (the design research/2026-10-08-jarl-field-report-and-repository-names/design.md, Proposed changes, built on a field report from a loop in another project): beyond the Jarl scope you approved in a-002, the design proposes (1) in 246, that a merge no longer records its CI as pending by default but only when a run will report, that a merge which lands while its check is red for a cause outside the branch is recorded with --red naming the issue that tracks the cause, and that whether a repository lands on its check is derived from its merge rows instead of a declared Check line; (2) in 244, that one answer can settle several ratify items with one verdict, and that superseding a ruling names the open issues that still cite it; (3) in 250, that the sentence "a compaction is a session start" also goes into the instructions Jarl's MCP server gives the host; (4) issue 280, that show prints by default only what the done gate counts (every row and verdict since the last start, every unanswered finding) and show --full the whole file. Options: (a) all four into 6.2.0: the Jarl part of the release grows, and 247 stays the first item to set aside; (b) none, they go to 6.3.0: until then a merge's CI stays pending or none, both false for a merge that is not pushed, an outside red is charged to the worker as a round, and show grows with 248's full reviews; (c) choose item by item. Recommendation (the architect's design): (a); it proposes every item for 6.2.0 and names the bounded show explicitly, because 248, already in 6.2.0, makes show grow faster.

- 2026-10-11 20:26 · decided hooks-silent-while-refreshing · by jarl
- 2026-10-11 20:26 · 228 evidence · Ruling hooks-silent-while-refreshing: While a hook-started refresh runs, the edit-time hooks answer nothing; they emit no status marker. The acceptance allows the rest to wait or answer with a marker, and the release plan's defaults allow a marker or silence while refreshing, never the old index. A marker on every edit would put a line into the agent's context on each tool call for the seconds or minutes a refresh takes, and SessionStart already says that the hooks are silent while the index is behind. Waiting would stall the edit. Silence is therefore the answer, and the documentation says so.

- 2026-10-11 20:33 · 234 review changes · by rev-runes-235-opus (fresh) · Important: src/relations/diagnostics.mts:48-52 places every JSON.parse failure whose message names no position at offset 0, and V8 names none for an unexpected token where a value belongs (a single-quoted string, an unquoted value, undefined), so such a failure is reported at line 1, column 1 wherever it is: at the tip a tsconfig with ['./src/*'] in single quotes on line 4 and a package.json with main: ./src/index.ts unquoted on line 2 both report line 1, column 1; that is not best effort but a wrong position, against the fields' own 'when known' (diagnostics.mts:7,9), the acceptance's 'with the file and position', docs/api.md:69 'where the failure lies' and autonomy-quality's more honest message, and Y5 and G9 will print it as the location; round 2 also swapped the workspace-member fixture (test/relations/diagnostics.test.mjs:80, from an unquoted value to a missing colon), so no test meets this case any more. Fix: when the parser names no position (other than an end of input) report the file without line and column, with a message that names no position (the type already makes both optional), or locate the token yourself; keep an unquoted-value fixture asserting it; reword docs/api.md:69 · Minor: docs/api.md:16 still lists only the error-document code, ELOCKED, ELOCKBREAK and usage as stable codes; plan section 8 puts config-unreadable beside them · Minor: src/relations/extractors/typescript-resolve.mts:416-475 parseJsoncAt builds a per-character offset map on every read, callback or not (5000 parses of a 9 KB package.json: about 920 ms against 630 ms at the base), against diagnostics.mts:22 'the resolvers skip the work'; build the map only on the failure path · Minor: typescript-resolve.mts:566 strips a literal invisible U+FEFF in its regex where :586 spells it as an escape · Minor: behind a byte order mark the column on line 1 counts the mark, one more than an editor shows · Verified: the round-2 fixes are real: at 6cf84e9 the BOM composer.json and compile_commands.json test, the BOM position test and the failureOffset test fail, at the tip all 17 diagnostics tests pass; one report for 50 imports, at line 6 through a comment and a trailing comma; jsconfig; a workspace member's package.json only when a bare import reaches it; composer once for both lookups; both compile_commands files; pnpm-workspace.yaml: three unreadable shapes report, six readable ones do not, and the reading is unchanged; the disk path without a view; the Before-you-upgrade line for BOM composer.json and compile_commands.json is in the changelog fragment for RF; the Y5 and G9 halves stay open by plan · Model: opus-5.5

- 2026-10-11 20:37 · 234 evidence · round 3 (commit 43124eb), Model: sonnet-5.5. ran: node --test test/relations/diagnostics.test.mjs before the fix with the new cases (a tsconfig with ['./src/*'] on line 4, a package.json with an unquoted main on line 2, an unquoted workspace-member value restored, the BOM column, failureOffset locating a token, a position-less report) · saw: pass 13, fail 6 (line 1 column 1 reported for both real cases; BOM column 8 instead of 7); after: pass 19, fail 0, with the tsconfig at line 4 column 24, the package.json at line 2 column 11, the BOM failure at column 7. Design: the parser names no position for an unexpected token at a value, so the reader scans for the first token that cannot be JSON and reports that place; where nothing is found the diagnostic has no line and column and a message that names none. docs/api.md lists config-unreadable among the stable codes and describes the position as it now is. Perf: 5000 parses of an 8.2 KB package.json through parseJsonc took 777, 757, 736 ms before and 551, 588, 592 ms after (the offset map is built only after a parse has failed). The invisible U+FEFF in the pnpm regex is now an escape. npm run check exit 0 (1771 pass, dist fresh); npm run coverage exit 0 (1771 pass; lines 98.98, branches 94.74, functions 99.05).

- 2026-10-11 20:44 · 003 review changes · by rev-grain-003-opus (fresh) · Important: the check now refuses statement scopes as method names. Grain gives auto.nameshape to every named method-kind scope (scopes.mjs:481-484), which includes PHP catch_clause (catch (Exception $e)) and Java/Groovy enhanced_for_statement (for (String x : xs)); NNT carries those node types (partition.mjs:343), so a camelCase method rule refuses every PHP catch variable and any deviating for-each variable (probe on synthetic files: the tip refuses Svc.php:6 $e and Owner.java:4 PET_NAME, the release base refuses neither). in the one-commit case the newest entry is the first parent's own, which can never have been written for the combination and could always count as owed; CHANGELOG.md:53 and docs/the-lock.md:181 name only a writer's clock set ahead · Minor (residual, file once with the merge case) — git merge --squash feature then yg check --approve before the commit records the branch's newest entry as the baseline and the committed squash is green; 189's merge --no-commit has the same window · Minor — the gate keys working-tree entries against git's blobs without normalizing line endings, so with the log checked out CRLF (a repository without the init's log.md text eol=lf line) the rebase passes with log-cycle-open; log-integrity normalizes, this and the merge-side check do not · Minor — an entry amended into the rebased commit reads as replayed like the squash-commit case the CHANGELOG names, and a combining commit dated ahead of the entry written for it (a script in the same second, a clock ahead where the rebase ran) asks for one more entry; the whole-second rounding itself is sound, it fails red and never green · Minor — 7c5a45b20 carries 221's review fix and 242 in one commit · Note — clearing approve from the model of the first worker (reviewer-rule-on-clearing-approve)

- 2026-10-12 01:05 · 014 review changes · by rev-runes-014-opus (fresh) · Important: a real edge that the pass without reach has is lost when a reference path spells a directory in another case than the repository (a Windows or macOS author): findFile (src/relations/extractors/csharp-reach.mts:144-151) folds the case of the last segment only, on a case-insensitive disk isFile accepts the raw spelling so the wrong-cased directory goes into refs (:218) and never equals the project of the target file, and a ProjectReference to a path the view does not hold is ignored instead of read as open (pinned by test/relations/csharp-reach.test.mjs:97-103), so the project stays closed and the target reads unreachable. Probe in catalogue form (src/Billing/Billing.csproj with ProjectReference Include="..\shared\Shared.csproj", the project in src/Shared/, src/Billing/Invoice.cs naming Acme.Shared.Clock): the base 2cebb0c gives src/Billing/Invoice.cs:3 -> Shared on disk and through the listed view, c9144f8 gives silence on both. The same exact-case lookup decides the Directory.Build.* names (:195) and Import paths (:264). This breaks the promises of docs/api.md:71 (The worst case is therefore the answer without reach), the module header (:17) and the 014 changelog line (so nothing real is lost). - 2026-10-18 22:55 · 332 review approve · by rev-g-note-opus (fresh) · Fresh-session Opus review of the design note only (no access to the author's reasoning; the work is a design, not code). Verified against JarlSkill-merge 0afdb65 and scratch loops. Reproduced: frozen 6.2.0 copy agrees 337/337 statuses, 246/246 Merged+CI, 250/250 Branch; probe A, B (log forgery accepted by set done) and C (Branch deleted by hand, coordinator approve closes) all reproduce; line refs spot-checked (2491, 2606, 1573, 1559/928, 1766) correct; record.appendLog is a wrapper so refusing there leaves L.appendLog and every command writer intact; Horde's two appendLog callers and editTicket/setTicketField write only non-state text; renderIssue is used only by new, so no writer re-renders a header from folded fields; views stay ~0.1 s. No Critical or Important. Minor: (1) cmdSet/cmdMerged/cmdNew write the header before the log line (1584/1591, 1771/1774, 1332/1335): a crash leaves a projection ahead of the record; say so and consider log-first. (2) Settling is not neutral: set appends a synthetic move and re-stamps Since; merged --sha --keep from the CLI drops CI and repeats the fresh-review note. (3) Profile drift beyond initial: a hand-edited statuses list changes movesInto, so past moves stop or start parsing and the views now follow. (4) Union merge orders ours-then-theirs, not by stamp; two clones merging in opposite directions fold differently; report out-of-stamp-order lines. (5) No tool path clears a mistaken Branch once journalled; state it. (6) Since gives no gate value and risks byte drift on older loops; could stay file-only. (7) Untracked fallback is ambiguous for an issue with merge/CI lines but no filing or move. (8) After, Resources and declared fields have log lines yet stay file-authoritative; say those lines are audit. (9) Oracle: add show per issue and check to the goldens, Horde's own suite at 326, and a Horde-shaped real loop; exported writeAtomic can still rewrite log.md whole (I1b scope).
